The proposed initial-capture counterexample\n+below is independent; it is not claimed to explain the six origin orphan rows.\n+\n+Existing physical ownership chain\n+spawnOwnedVerificationTool creates a detached monitor and retains its ChildProcess,\n+stdin lifeline, IPC terminal, and completed promise. The monitor creates a separate\n+detached selected tool group, owns that child handle, closes it on lifeline EOF,\n+and publishes terminal only after its own bounded kill/observation. Its finally then\n+SIGKILLs the inner group immediately. failAfterObservedCleanup sees an empty set,\n+does not perform any observation, and rejects with the first error. This destroys\n+the still-available parent chain before attempting the existing frozen snapshot.\n+The case is finite: original root and monitor/tool parent chain remain live and\n+observable after one transient initial native inspection failure.\n+\n+Minimal proposed implementation shape\n+1. Do not signal guessed detached groups.\n+3. Within the SAME original termination deadline, attempt the existing bounded\n+   freeze-confirmation observation; successful actual snapshots must immediately\n+   add their observed descendant groups even though a primary error is pending.\n+4. Confirm the root-group rows are actually frozen before describing capture as\n+   successful. Its existing\n+   failure-drain then waits for the now-known groups and their natural monitor\n+   lifeline cleanup, still within the original deadline, before surfacing the error.\n+6. If the recovery freeze/inspection also fails, preserve both original causes\n+   (AggregateError whose cause remains primary). Do not swallow first failure,\n+   retry indefinitely, reset the deadline, or report complete inventory/drain.\n+\n+First formal native business red, using existing public supervisor API\n+Extend the held-monitor reclamation fixture with an initial-capture failure case.\n+Its real selected tool publishes actual PID/child PID/PGIDs/monitor PID. At the\n+initial-capture seam, the fixture performs a native ps observation, verifies actual\n+tool -> monitor -> inner ancestry and recorded PGIDs, and pauses exactly that\n+observed owned monitor. This successful observation is an independent oracle; it\n+is explicitly NOT returned to the supervisor. Then invoke the original /bin/ps\n+with an invalid column, catch its actual Error object, and throw that same object.\n+\n+The old implementation kills the inner root and rejects with an empty production\n+inventory while the controlled monitor remains paused. At caller catch, actual\n+native observations deterministically show the previously captured monitor/tool\n+still live. -0,0 +1,63 @@\n+namespace Wanxiangshu.Persistence.Journal\n+\n+open Wanxiangshu.Foundation\n+open Wanxiangshu.Foundation.Identity\n+\n+module JournalOutcome =\n+\n+    type JournalFailure =\n+        | WriteFailed of reason: string\n+        | FlushFailed of reason: string\n+\n+    /// A writer that never attempted the caller's event. This is deliberately\n+    /// distinct from CommitUnknown: lifecycle refusal is known-not-committed.\n+    type JournalUnavailable =\n+        | WriterPoisoned of firstFailure: string\n+        | WriterClosing\n+        | WriterDisposed\n+\n+    /// PERSIST-002: physical append is committed or uncertain. Semantic cuts and\n+    /// lifecycle refusal are both known-not-committed and stay explicit instead\n+    /// of being collapsed into physical uncertainty.\n+    type CommitResult<'e> =\n+        | Committed of 'e\n+        /// Bytes are durable, but the business rule cut this event and immediately\n+        /// wrote a durable reset fact. This invocation failed; the writer remains usable.\n+        | Rejected of EventId * reason: string\n+        | NotAttempted of EventId * JournalUnavailable\n+        | CommitUnknown of EventId * JournalFailure\n+\n+    /// Physical fate of a single journal append.\n+    ///\n+    /// WriteUnknown = durable-state indeterminate; WriterUnavailable = the writer\n+    /// refused without attempting (known-not-committed); FactRejected = the line\n+    /// bytes are durable but the semantic fold cut it — the append boundary treats\n+    /// it as fatal evidence rather than retryable.\n+    type JournalAppendFailure =\n+        | WriteUnknown of EventId * JournalFailure\n+        | WriterUnavailable of EventId * JournalUnavailable\n+        | FactRejected of EventId * FoldRejection\n+\n+    module JournalAppendFailure =\n+\n+        let describe (failure: JournalAppendFailure) : string =\n+            match failure with\n+            | WriteUnknown(eventId, WriteFailed reason) ->\n+                sprintf \"append outcome unknown for %s: write failed: %s\" (EventId.value eventId) reason\n+            | WriteUnknown(eventId, FlushFailed reason) ->\n+                sprintf \"append outcome unknown for %s: flush failed: %s\" (EventId.value eventId) reason\n+            | WriterUnavailable(eventId, WriterPoisoned firstFailure) ->\n+                sprintf\n+                    \"append not attempted for %s: writer poisoned by prior failure: %s\"\n+                    (EventId.value eventId)\n+                    firstFailure\n+            | WriterUnavailable(eventId, WriterClosing) ->\n+                sprintf \"append not attempted for %s: writer is closing\" (EventId.value eventId)\n+            | WriterUnavailable(eventId, WriterDisposed) ->\n+                sprintf \"append not attempted for %s: writer is disposed\" (EventId.value eventId)\n+            | FactRejected(eventId, rejection) ->\n+                sprintf\n+                    \"journal semantic cut at %s: fact \\'%s\\' rejected: %s\"\n+                    (EventId.value eventId)\n+                    rejection.Fact\n+
